The more squarely you look at them, the more harmless they become. I ask you to subject them to the most careful and severe scrutiny, entirely confident as to the result.

“The state will tell you, undoubtedly, that in spite of what you have heard, the fact remains that Susan Ives and Stephen Bellamy had the means, the motive, and the opportunity to commit this crime. It is our contention that they had nothing of the kind. No weapon has been traced to either of them; it would have been to all intents and purposes physically impossible for them to reach the gardener’s cottage, execute this murder and return to Stephen Bellamy’s house between the time that the gasoline vender saw them leave Lakedale and the time that Orsini saw them arrive at Mr. Bellamy’s home﻿—a scant forty minutes, according to the outside figures of their own witnesses; not quite twenty-five according to ours.

===Chinese===

“本州的证词不但与我们的一方不相冲突——它印证了我们一方。被偷听的电话，书房里的刀，染污的法兰绒外套，造访斯蒂芬·贝拉米家，小屋外泥地上的车辙，屋内灯上的指纹——那是本州的案情，诸位先生，也正是我们的案情。这些狰狞的事实，这些本州手里威风而可怕的武器，在真相的清白光亮之下，变成了一组极简单、极合理、且势所必然的情状，桩桩有说明，事事有交代。你们端详得越正，它们就越无害。我请你们对它们施以最审慎最严厉的检视，而对结果，我全无半点担心。

“本州无疑会对你们说：任凭你们听了这许多，苏珊·艾夫斯与斯蒂芬·贝拉米具备作案的手段、动机与机会，事实俱在。我方的主张是：他们一样也不具备。没有任何一件凶器追到他们二人头上；从卖汽油的伙计看见他们离开雷克代尔，到奥尔西尼看见他们抵达贝拉米先生家——按他们自己证人的最宽算法不过区区四十分钟，按我们的证人不足二十五分钟——要在其间赶到花匠小屋、行凶、再赶回贝拉米先生家，实际上是生理上不可能的。

===Original===

“But take the absolute substantiated forty-minute limit﻿—from 9:15 to 9:55. You are asked to believe that in that time they hurled themselves in a small rickety car over ten miles, possibly more, of unfamiliar roads in total darkness, took a rough dirt cutoff, groped their way through the back gates of the Thorne place to the little road that led to the cottage, got out, entered the cottage, became involved in a bitter and violent scene with Mimi Bellamy which culminated in her death by murder; remained there long enough to map out a campaign which involved removing her jewels from her dead body, while fabricating an elaborate alibi﻿—and also long enough to permit Mr. Thorne, who has arrived on the piazza, ample time to get well on his way; came out, got back into the invisible automobile and arrived at Mr. Bellamy’s house, three miles away, at five minutes to ten. Gentlemen, does this seem to you credible? I confess that it seems to me so incredible﻿—so fantastically, so grotesquely incredible﻿—that I am greatly inclined to offer you an apology for going into it at such length. So much for the means, so much for the opportunity; now for the motive.

“There, I think, we touch the weakest point in the state’s case against these two. That the state itself fully grasps its weakness, I submit, is adduced from the fact that not one witness they have put on the stand has been asked a single question that would tend to establish either of the motives ascribed to them by the state﻿—widely differing motives, alike only in their monstrous absurdity. It is the state’s contention, if it still cleaves to the theory originally advanced, that Madeleine Bellamy was murdered by Susan Ives because she feared poverty, and that she was aided and abetted by Stephen Bellamy in this bloody business because he was crazed by jealousy.

“I ask you to consider these two propositions with more gravity and concentration than they actually merit, because on your acceptance or rejection of them depends your acceptance or rejection of the guilt of these two. You cannot dismiss them as too absurd for any earthly consideration. You cannot say, ‘Oh, of course that wasn’t the reason they killed her, but that’s not our concern; there may have been another reason that we don’t know anything about.’ No, fortunately for us, you cannot do that.

===Chinese===

“就算采信那铁定的四十分钟上限——九点一刻到九点五十五。你们被要求相信：他们在这一段时间里，驾一辆摇摇晃晃的小车，在一片漆黑中冲过十英里上下、或许更多的陌生道路，抄一条坎坷的土近道，摸黑穿过索恩家庄园的后门，上那条通小屋的小路，下车进屋，同米米·贝拉米卷入一场激烈狂暴的冲突，以她被杀告终；留在现场久到足以商定一套方案——包括从她尸体上摘下珠宝，同时编一套周密的不在场证明——也久到足以让已到门廊的索恩先生从容走远；然后出来，钻进那辆能隐能现的汽车，于九点五十五分抵达三英里外贝拉米先生的家。诸位先生，这听着可信吗？我承认，它在我看来不可信到这步田地——荒诞到、怪诞到不可信的地步——以致我颇有几分想为方才这一番铺陈向诸位致歉。手段讲完了，机会讲完了；现在讲动机。

“这一项，我想我们触到了本州指控这两人的最弱一环。本州自己深知己弱，我以为可以由此推知：他们请上证人席的证人，没有一个被问到过一个字，足以确立他们加给这两人的任一动机——两个动机大相径庭，只在骇人的荒唐上彼此相像。本州的主张——倘若它还抱着起初提出的那套理论——是说：玛德琳·贝拉米之死于苏珊·艾夫斯之手，是因为她怕穷；而斯蒂芬·贝拉米之所以在这桩血案里协从，是因为妒火焚心。

“我请诸位以比它们实际配得的更郑重、更专注的态度掂量这两条命题，因为对它们的取舍，系着你们对这两人罪责的取舍。你们不能把它们当作荒唐得不值一哂而挥开。你们不能说：‘哦，那当然不是他们杀她的缘故，可那不归我们管；兴许另有缘故，只是我们不知道。’不，幸而对我们来说，你们不能那样做。”

===Original===

“These, preposterous as they are, are the only motives suggested; they are the least preposterous ones that the state could find to submit to you. If you are not able to accept them the state’s case crumbles to pieces before your eyes. If you look at it attentively for as much as thirty seconds, I believe that you will see it crumbling. What you are asked to believe is this: That for the most sordid, base, mercenary and calculating motives﻿—the desire to protect her financial future from possible hazard﻿—Susan Ives committed a cruel, wicked, and bloody murder.

“For two hours you listened to Susan Ives speaking to you from that witness box. If you can believe that she is sordid, base, calculating, mercenary, cruel, and bloody, I congratulate you. Such power of credulity emerges from the ranks of mere talent into those of sheer genius.

“Stephen Bellamy, you are told, was her accomplice﻿—driven stark, staring, raving mad by the most bestial, despicable, and cowardly form of jealousy. You have heard Stephen Bellamy, too, from that witness box, telling you of the anguish of despair that filled him when he thought that harm had befallen his beloved﻿—if you can believe that he is despicable, cowardly, bestial, and mad, then undoubtedly you are still able to believe in a world tenanted by giants and fairies and ogres and witches and dragons. Not one of them would be so strange a phenomenon as the transformation of this adoring, chivalrous, and restrained gentleman into the base villain that you are asked to accept.

“The state’s case, gentlemen! It crumbles, does it not? It crumbles before your eyes. Means, motives, opportunity﻿—look at them steadily and clearly and they vanish into thin air.

“If means, motives, and opportunity constitute a basis for an accusation of murder, this trial might well end in several arrests that would be as fully justified as the arrests of Susan Ives and Stephen Bellamy. I make no such accusations; I am strong and sure and safe enough in the proved innocence of these two to feel no need of summoning others to the bar of justice. That is neither my duty nor my desire, but it would be incompatible with the desire for abstract truth not to point out that far stronger hypothetical cases might be made out against several whose paths also have crossed the path of the ill-starred girl who died in that cottage.

===Chinese===

“这两条命题，再荒唐，也是仅有被提出的动机；也是本州找得出来呈给诸位的最不荒唐的动机。你们若不能接受它们，本州的案子便在你们眼前碎为齑粉。你们只要正眼端详它三十秒，我相信就会看见它在碎。你们被要求相信的是：为着最肮脏、最下贱、最见钱眼开、最深于算计的动机——保护自己的钱财前景免遭可能的危难——苏珊·艾夫斯犯下了一桩残忍、邪恶、血腥的谋杀。

“两个钟头，你们听苏珊·艾夫斯在那个证人席上对你们讲话。你们若能相信她肮脏、下贱、算计、贪利、残忍而嗜血，我向你们道贺。这样的轻信之力，已经超出才能的行列，跻身纯粹的天才之列了。

“斯蒂芬·贝拉米呢，你们被告知，他是她的帮凶——被一种最兽性、最卑鄙、最怯懦的妒忌逼得疯了。你们也从那个证人席上听过斯蒂芬·贝拉米，听他讲他以为爱人遭祸时那填满他的绝望的苦痛——你们若能相信他卑鄙、怯懦、兽性而疯狂，那么毫无疑问，你们仍能相信一个住着巨人、仙女、食人魔、女巫和龙的世界。他们中间随便哪一个，都没有你们被要求接受的那件事怪异：把这位一往情深、侠义克己的绅士，变成那号下流恶棍。”

===Original===

“We come as close to establishing as perfect an alibi as it is likely that innocent people, little suspecting that one will be called for, would be able to establish. What alibi had practically anyone who has appeared against these two for that night? The knife that Dr. Stanley described to you might have been one of various types﻿—such a knife as might have been well discovered in a tool chest, in a kitchen drawer, in the equipment of a sportsman.

“You have analyzed the motives ascribed to the defendants. I submit that, taken at random, three somewhat solider motives might be robbery or blackmail or drunken jealousy. When one possible witness removes himself to Canada, when another takes his life﻿—they are safely out of reach of our jurisdiction, but not beyond the scope of our speculations. I submit that these specifications are at least fruitful of interest. Abandoning them, however, I suggest to you that that girl, young, beautiful, fragile, and unprotected in that isolated cottage with jewels at her throat and on her fingers, was the natural prey of any nameless beast roving in the neighbourhood﻿—one who had possibly stalked her from the time that she left her house, one who had possibly been prowling through the grounds of this deserted estate on some business, sinister or harmless. Ostensibly this was a case of murder for robbery; it remains still the simplest and most natural explanation﻿—too simple and too natural by half for a brilliant prosecutor, an ambitious police force, and frenzied public, all clamouring for a victim.

“Well, they have had their victims; I hope that they do not sleep worse at night for the rest of their lives when they think of the victims that they selected.

===Chinese===

“本州的案子，诸位先生！它碎了，是不是？它在你们眼前碎。手段、动机、机会——正眼看清它们，它们便化为一缕青烟。

“倘若手段、动机加机会就够立一桩杀人指控，这场审判大可以再捕几个人才是，其正当性与逮捕苏珊·艾夫斯、斯蒂芬·贝拉米一般无二。我作不出这样的指控；我对这两人的清白已证明得足够坚实、足够安稳，无须再传别人上正义之席。那既非我的职分，也非我的意愿；可是，若不指出还有几位的路径同样横穿过那死在小屋里的薄命姑娘的路径、对他们可以立起假想中远为强硬的案子，便与求真之心不相容。这几位的假想案情，姑且立此存照。

“我们几乎立起了清白者所能立起的那样完满的不在场证明——清白者何曾料到会用得上它。那一晚，凡是出庭作证反对这两人的，又有谁实际上拿出了不在场证明？斯坦利医生向你们描述的那把刀，可能属于好几种形制——那样的刀，工具箱里翻得出，厨房抽屉里翻得出，猎人的行头里翻得出。

“你们已经剖析过加给被告的动机。我且说，随手拈来，还有三个硬些的：劫财、敲诈、酒后妒杀。当一个可能的证人自我流放到加拿大，另一个结果了自己的性命——他们逃出了我们的管辖，却逃不出我们的推想。我且说，这几条至少耐人寻味。搁下它们不谈，我提示诸位：那姑娘，年轻、美丽、娇弱、无人护佑，孤身在那座孤零零的小屋里，颈上指上皆着珠宝，正是任何一头在附近游荡的无名兽类的天然猎物——那兽或许从她离家起就缀上了她，或许本就在这座无人庄园的场院里逡巡，干着某桩营生，凶也罢，无伤也罢。表面上这是一桩劫杀；它至今仍是最简单、最自然的解释——对一位野心勃勃的检察官、一支立功心切的警队和一群喧嚣着索要牺牲品的狂热公众来说，只是简单自然得过了头。

“好，牺牲品他们已经有了；但愿他们往后余生夜夜想起自己挑中的牺牲品时，不至睡得更糙。”

===Original===

“Two things the state has made no attempt to explain﻿—who it was that stole the note from Patrick Ives’s study and who it was that laughed when Madeleine Bellamy screamed. Whoever took the note, it was not Susan Ives. She had no possible motive in denying having taken it; she freely admitted that she searched the study for some proof of her husband’s duplicity, and she also admitted that Elliot Farwell had informed her that he believed her husband was meeting Madeleine Bellamy at the cottage that very night. The note, which we presume was making a rendezvous, would in no way have added to her previous information. Any one of six or eight servants or six or eight guests may have intercepted it; whoever did so knew when and where Madeleine Bellamy was to be found that night.

“The laugh is more baffling and disconcerting still; the state must find it mightily so. It will be instructive to see whether they are going to ask you to believe that it was uttered by Stephen Bellamy as he saw his wife fall. In my opinion only a degenerate or a drunken monster would have chosen that moment for mirth. Possibly it is Mr. Farr’s contention that he was both. Providentially, that is for you and not for him to decide.

“The state has still another little matter to explain to your satisfaction. According to its theory, Stephen Bellamy and Susan Ives arrived at the scene of the crime in a car﻿—in Mr. Bellamy’s car. The murderer of Madeleine Bellamy did not arrive in a car﻿—or at any rate, no car was visible two minutes later in that vicinity. There were no tire tracks in the space behind the house, and the state’s own witnesses have proved that on both Stephen Bellamy’s visits his car was left squarely in front of the cottage door. If someone left an unlighted car parked somewhere down the main drive, as the state contends, it was not he. His car would have been clearly visible to any human being who approached the cottage. It will, as I say, be instructive to see how the state disposes of this vital fact.

===Chinese===

“有两件事，本州没有作过任何解释的努力——是谁从帕特里克·艾夫斯的书房偷走了那张条子，又是谁在玛德琳·贝拉米尖叫时笑出了声。拿条子的人，无论如何不是苏珊·艾夫斯。她否认拿过，不可能有任何动机；她痛快承认，她为找丈夫负心的凭据搜过书房；她也痛快承认，埃利奥特·法韦尔告诉过她，她丈夫当晚就要在小屋会见玛德琳·贝拉米。那张想来是约会的条子，丝毫无补于她已得的情报。六八个佣人、六八位客人，谁都可能截下它；截下它的人，便知道那晚何时、何地能找到玛德琳·贝拉米。

“那声笑更扑朔、更慑人；本州必定觉得它棘手得很。且看他们是不是要请你们相信，那笑出自斯蒂芬·贝拉米之口，在他眼见妻子倒下的当儿。依我看，只有堕落的变态或醉醺醺的怪物，才会挑那一刻取乐。或许法尔先生的主张是：他两样都是。所幸，这由你们裁断，不由他。

“本州还有一件小事要向诸位交代清楚。依照它的理论，斯蒂芬·贝拉米和苏珊·艾夫斯是乘车抵达罪案现场的——乘贝拉米先生的车。杀害玛德琳·贝拉米的凶手不是乘车抵达的——至少两分钟后那一带看不见任何车。屋后那片空地上没有车辙，而本州自己的证人已经证明，贝拉米先生两度到访，车都端端正正停在小屋门口。倘若如本州所主张，有人把一辆熄了灯的车停在主车道旁什么地方，那绝不是他。他的车，任何一个走近小屋的人都看得一清二楚。我说过，且看本州如何处置这个攸关的事实。”

===Original===

“I have touched on these matters because I have desired to make clear to you two or three factors that are absolutely incompatible with any theory that the state has advanced. If they are to be disposed of in the most remotely plausible fashion, some other theory must be evolved, and I believe that you will agree with me that it is rather late in the day to produce another theory. I have not touched on them﻿—and I wish to make this perfectly clear﻿—on the ground that they are in no way necessary to our defense. That defense is not dependent on such intriguing details as who took the note, or who laughed, or whether the murderer approached his goal on foot or in a car. The defense that I advance is simple and straightforward and independent of any other circumstances.

“Of all the things that I have said to you, there is only one that I hold it essential that you carry in the very core of your memory when you leave this room on as solemn an errand as falls to the lot of any man. This only: That the sole defense that I plead for Stephen Bellamy and Susan Ives is that they are innocent﻿—as entirely and unequivocally innocent as any man of you in whose hands rests their fate; that this foul and brutal murder was against their every wish, hope, or desire; that it is to them as ghastly, as incredible, and as mysterious as it is to you. That and that only is their defense.

“It is not my task, as you know﻿—as in time Judge Carver will tell you﻿—to prove them innocent. It is the state’s to prove them guilty. A heavy task they will find it, I most truly believe. But I would have you find them something more than not guilty. That is the verdict that you may render with your lips, but with your hearts I ask you to render another more generous and ungrudgingly. ‘Innocent’﻿—a lovely, valiant, and fearless word, a word untainted by suspicion or malice. A verdict that has no place in any court, but I believe that all who hear your lips pronounce “Not guilty” will read it in your eyes. I pray that they may.

“I said to you that when you left this room you would be bound on the most solemn of all errands. I say to you now that when you return you may well be bound on the most beautiful one imaginable﻿—you will return in order to give life to two who have stood in the shadow of death. Life!

===Chinese===

“我触及这些，是因为想让诸位看清两三个因素，它们与本州提出的任何理论都绝不相容。若要把它们处置得稍微像样，就得另立一套理论；而我想诸位会同意我：事到如今，另立理论未免太晚。我触及它们——这一点我要说透——绝不是因为我们的辩护有半分需要。这辩护不依赖谁拿了条子、谁笑了、凶手是步行还是乘车赴约这一类引人入胜的细枝末节。我提出的辩护简单、直捷、不倚傍任何别的情状。

“在我对诸位说过的一切里，只有一件，我认为至关重要，请你们把它放在记忆最深处带出这间屋子——你们此去肩负的，是人间最庄重的差事。只有这一件：我为斯蒂芬·贝拉米与苏珊·艾夫斯所申辩的唯一辩护，就是他们无辜——如你们中任何一位握着他们命运的人一样，完全而无保留地无辜；这桩污秽凶残的谋杀，与他们的每一个愿望、每一线希望、每一个欲念都相反；它之于他们，同之于你们一样狰狞、一样匪夷所思、一样神秘。这，也唯有这，才是他们的辩护。

“你们知道——不久卡弗法官也会告诉你们——证明他们无辜不是我的任务。证明他们有罪，是本州的任务。我真心相信，那是他们会发现的一副重担。可我要你们给他们的，不止于‘无罪’。那是你们可以用嘴唇宣判的裁决；而我请你们用你们的心，再给一个更宽厚、更不吝惜的。‘无辜’——一个可爱的、勇毅的、无畏的词，一个不染嫌疑与恶意的词。任何法庭的律条里都没有它的位置，可我相信，凡听见你们唇间宣出‘无罪’的人，都会在你们眼睛里读到它。我祈祷他们读得到。

“我对你们说过，你们离开这间屋子时，肩负的是所有差事中最庄重的一件。我现在对你们说：你们回来时，肩负的恐怕是所能想象的最美的一件——你们回来，是为了把生命交还给两个一直站在死亡阴影里的人。生命！

===Original===

“You cannot give back to Susan Ives something that she has lost﻿—a golden faith and carefree security, a confidence in this world and all its works. You cannot give back to Stephen Bellamy the dead girl who was his treasure and delight, about whose bright head clustered all his dreams. You cannot give back to them much that made life sweetest, but, gentlemen, you can give them life. You can restore to them the good earth, the clean air, the laughter of children, the hands of love, starlight and firelight and sunlight and moonlight﻿—and brightest of all, the light of home shining through windows long dark. All these things you hold in your hands. All these things are yours to give. Gentlemen, I find it in my heart to envy you greatly that privilege, to covet greatly that opportunity.”

He sat down, slowly and heavily, and through the room there ran an eager murmur of confidence and ease, a swift slackening of tension, a shifting of suspense. And as though in answer to it, Farr was on his feet. He stood silent for a moment, his hands clasped over the back of the chair before him, his eyes, brilliantly inscrutable, sweeping the upturned faces before him. When he lifted his voice, the familiar clang was muted:

“Your Honour, gentlemen, when my distinguished adversary rose to address you an hour or so ago, he assured you that he was about to take very little of your time. We would none of us grudge him one moment that he has subsequently taken. He is waging a grim and desperate battle, and moments and even hours seem infinitesimal weapons to interpose between those two whose defense is entrusted to him, and who stand this day in peril of their lives on the awful brink of eternity itself.

===Chinese===

“你们无法还给苏珊·艾夫斯她已失去的东西——金色的信念与无忧的安全，对这个世界及其一切的信任。你们无法还给斯蒂芬·贝拉米那位死去的姑娘——那是他的珍宝与欢喜，他所有的梦都簇拥在她明亮的头颅四周。生命里最甜的许多东西，你们无力归还；可是诸位先生，你们能给他们生命。你们能把好大地、干净空气、孩子的笑声、爱的手、星光火光阳光月光还给他们——还有最亮的那一样：家的灯火，透过久暗的窗子重新点亮。这一切都握在你们手里。这一切都由你们来给。诸位先生，说句心里话，我真是艳羡你们这份特权，眼热你们这个机会。”

他缓缓地、沉沉地坐下；满室掠过一阵信任与宽舒的低语，一阵紧张的驟然松弛，一阵悬念的挪移。仿佛在应答它，法尔起立了。他静立片刻，双手扣在面前椅背上，那双眼睛——亮而莫测——扫过面前一张张仰起的脸。开口时，那熟悉的锿锵低了哑：

“法官阁下，诸位先生，个把钟头之前，我杰出的对手起立向诸位致辞时，曾向诸位保证他只占用很少时间。他此后占去的每一刻，我们谁也不会吝惜。他打的是一场严峻绝望的仗；在受他辩护的那两个人——他们今日站在生死之间，站在永恒本身的可怕崖沿上——与他之间，时刻甚至时辰，都是渺小得可怜的屏障。”

===Original===

“The plea that he has made to you is as eloquent and moving a one as you will hear in many a long day; it is my misfortune that the one that I am about to make must follow hard on its heels, and will necessarily be shorn of both eloquence and emotion. It will be the shorter for lack of them, but not the better. What I lack in oratory I shall endeavour to supply in facts: facts too cold, hard, and grim to make pleasant hearing﻿—still, facts. It is my unwelcome duty to place them before you; I shall not shrink from it. It will not be necessary for me to elaborate on them. They will speak for themselves more eloquently than I could ever hope to do, and I propose to let them do so.

“Before I marshal them before you, I will dispose as briefly as possible of two or three issues that Mr. Lambert has seen fit to raise in his speech to you. First, as to the wealth of Mrs. Ives. I cannot see that the fact that she is wealthy is in any way a vital issue in this case, but Mr. Lambert evidently considered it sufficiently important to dwell on at considerable length. He managed very skilfully to place before you the picture of a modest little farmhouse with roses clambering over a cottage gate, presided over by an even more modest chatelaine. Very idyllic and utterly and absolutely misleading.

“The little farmhouse is a mansion of some twenty-odd rooms, the roses grow in a sunken garden as large as a small park; not many cottages boast a swimming pool, a tennis court, a bowling green and a garage for five cars﻿—but Mrs. Ives’s cottage took these simple improvements as a matter of course. Mr. Lambert drew your attention to the fact that if you had rung a doorbell the lady herself might have hastened to welcome your summons, and, he implies, to welcome you in to see how simply she lived.

===Chinese===

“他向诸位所作的恳求，是许久难得一听的雄辩感人之作；而不幸在于，我行将作出的这一篇必须紧随其后，也势必被削去雄辩与情感。缺了这两样，它会短一些，却不会更好。演说上的欠缺，我将尽力用事实来补足：太冷、太硬、太森然、不堪入耳的事实——但终究是事实。把它们摆在诸位面前，是我这份不讨喜的职责；我不会回避。也无须我多加铺陈。它们自会说话，比我所能希冀的更雄辩；我打算就让它们自说。

“在把它们列队摆到诸位面前之前，先尽可能简短地了结兰伯特先生在致辞中认为有必要提出的两三个论点。第一，关于艾夫斯太太的财富。我看不出她富有这一事实与本案有任何紧要关联，可兰伯特先生显然认为它重要得很，值得大谈特谈。他手法娴熟地在诸位面前铺开一幅画图：一座朴素的小农舍，玫瑰攀满柴门，主持其上的女主人比农舍还要朴素。非常有田园诗意，也彻头彻尾、完完全全地误导人。

“那座小农舍是一座二十多个房间的宅邸；玫瑰长在一座下陷式花园里，大得像个小公园；没有几家庄户能有游泳池、网球场、草坪保龄球场和五车位车库——可艾夫斯太太的庄户把这些简朴的改善视作理所当然。兰伯特先生提请诸位注意：若你们按响门铃，女主人兴许会亲自赶来应门；他还暗示，会迎你们进去看看她过得多么简朴。”

===Original===

“I doubt profoundly whether Mrs. Ives ever opened her door in her life unless she was intending to pass through it, and I doubt even more profoundly whether you would ever have been requested to cross the threshold of her home. Mr. Lambert did admit that the bell might have been answered by a little maid, but he failed to specify which one of the five little maids it might have been. He added, in an even more lyric vein, that Susan Ives had no more than any of your wives﻿—no more than roses in her garden, sunlight in her windows, babies in her nursery. I confess myself somewhat taken aback. Are your wives the possessors of an acre of roses, a hundred windows to let in sunshine, a day and night nursery for your babies to play in, with a governess in still a third room to supervise their play? If such is the case, you are fortunate indeed.

“As for Stephen Bellamy, Mr. Lambert has assured you that any mechanic in the land was as well off as he. Well, possibly. The mechanics that I know don’t have maids to help their pretty wives, and gardeners to sleep over their pretty garages, but perhaps the ones that you know do.

“So much for the wealth of the defendants. I said at the outset that it was a matter of no great importance, and in one sense it is not; in a deeper sense, it is of the greatest possible significance. Not that Susan Ives was, in the strictest sense of the word, a wealthy woman, but because of the alchemy that had been wrought in her by the sinister magic of what we may call the golden touch.

“You all know the legend of Midas, I am sure﻿—the tale of that unhappy king who wished that every object that his fingers rested on might turn to gold, and whose fingers strayed one day to his little daughter’s hair and transformed her into a small statue﻿—beautiful, shining, brilliant, but cold and hard and inhuman as metal itself. Long ago Curtiss Thorne’s fingers must have rested on his little daughter’s hair, and what he made of his child then the woman is today. The product of pride, of power, of privilege, of riches﻿—Susan Ives, proud, powerful, privileged, and rich﻿—the golden girl, a charming object of luxury in the proper surrounding, a useless encumbrance out of them.

===Chinese===

“我深深怀疑，艾夫斯太太此生可曾为自己开过门——除非她正要穿门而出；我更深深怀疑，诸位是否真会被请去跨进她家的门槛。兰伯特先生倒也承认，应门的也许是个小女仆，只是他没有说明，那是五个小女仆中的哪一个。他还以更抒情的笔调补充说，苏珊·艾夫斯拥有的不比你们哪位的妻子更多——不过是园中的玫瑰、窗里的阳光、育儿室里的娃娃。我承认自己有些愕然。诸位的妻子，可是都拥有一英亩玫瑰、一百扇迎阳光的窗、一昼一夜两间育儿室给娃娃玩耍，外加第三间房里的家庭女教师看着他们玩？若果真如此，诸位真是好福气。

“至于斯蒂芬·贝拉米，兰伯特先生向诸位保证，全国的机械师过得都不比他差。好吧，也许。我认识的机械师，家里可没有女仆帮衬漂亮的太太，也没有园丁睡在漂亮的车库楼上——不过诸位认识的那类，兴许有。

“被告的财富，就说到这里。我开场便说过，这事无关紧要；就一层意义而言，确实如此；可在更深一层，它有着最大限度的重要性。不是因苏珊·艾夫斯严格说来算得上豪富，而是因为她身上被一种邪门的魔法——我们不妨称之为点金术——炼出来的那种质变。

“迈达斯的传说，诸位想必都熟悉——那位不幸的国王，祈愿手指触到的一切都化为黄金，有一天手指抚到小女儿的头发，把她变成了一尊小小的金像——美丽、闪亮、辉煌，却如金属本身一样冰冷、坚硬、非人。很久以前，柯蒂斯·索恩的手指想必也抚过小女儿的头发；他把孩子塑成什么，这个女人今日就是什么。傲慢、权势、特权、财富的产物——苏珊·艾夫斯，傲慢、有权、有特权、富有——黄金之女，在相称的环境里是一件迷人的奢侈品，出了那环境便是一件无用的累赘。”

===Original===

“No one knew this better than the golden girl herself﻿—she had had bitter cause to know it, remember; and on that fatal summer afternoon in June a drunken breath set the pedestal rocking beneath her feet. She moved swiftly down from that pedestal, with the firm intention of making it steady for all time. It is not the gold that we hold in our hands that is a menace and a curse, gentlemen﻿—not the shining counters that we may change for joy and beauty and health and mercy﻿—it is the cold metal that has grown into our hearts. I hold no brief against wealth itself. I hold a brief against the product of the Midas touch.

“Mr. Lambert next introduced to you most skilfully a very dangerous theme﻿—the theme of the deep personal interest that he takes in both defendants, more especially in Susan Ives. The sincerity of his devotion to her it is impossible to doubt. I for one am very far from doubting it. He loved the little girl before the fingers of Midas had rested heavy on her hair; he sees before him still only those bright curls of childhood clustering about an untarnished brow. Many of you who have daughters felt tears sting in your eyes when he told you that he loved her as his daughter﻿—I, who have none, felt the sting myself.

“But, gentlemen, I ask you only this: Are you, in all truth and fairness, the most unbiased judges of your daughter’s characters? Would you credit the word of an archangel straight from heaven who told you that your daughter was a murderess, if that daughter denied it? Never﻿—never, in God’s world, and you know it! If, in your hearts, you say to yourself, ‘He has known Susan Ives and loved her for many years; he loves her still, so she must be all he thinks,’ then Mr. Lambert’s warm eloquence will have accomplished its purpose and my cold logic will have failed.

“But I ask you, gentlemen, to use your heads and not your hearts. I ask you to discount heavily not Mr. Lambert’s sincerity, nor his affection, nor his eloquence, but his judgment and his credulity. Platitudes are generally the oldest and profoundest of truths; one of the most ancient and most profound of all is the axiom that Love is blind.

“So much for two general challenges that it has been my duty to meet; the more specific ones of the note, the car, and the laugh, I will deal with in their proper places. We are now through with generalizations and down to facts.

===Chinese===

“没有人比黄金之女本人更清楚这一点——记住，她是有惨痛的缘由才清楚的；而就在六月那个致命的夏日午后，一口酒气让她脚下的基座晃了起来。她迅疾地从基座上走下来，抱定一个坚定的意图：让它从此永远稳固。手中的黄金并不为祸为咒，诸位——不是那些闪亮的筹码，我们能拿它们去换欢乐、美、健康与恩慈——为祸为咒的，是长进心里的冷金属。我不与财富本身为敌。我与迈达斯之手的产物为敌。

“兰伯特先生接着又极为老练地向诸位引入了一个非常危险的主题——他对两名被告、尤其是对苏珊·艾夫斯怀有深厚私人情谊的主题。他对她的挚爱之诚挚，不容怀疑；至少我远远不至于怀疑它。在迈达斯的手指沉沉压上她的头发之前，他就爱着那个小女孩；如今他眼前看见的，依然只是童年的明亮鬈发簇拥着一块未染尘垢的额头。你们中间做父亲的，听见他说爱她如己女时，眼里都刺进了泪水——我无儿无女，也感到了那刺痛。

“可是诸位，我只问你们一句：说句公道话，你们当真是评判自己女儿品性时最不带偏见的人吗？倘若有个天使长直从天上来告诉你们，你们的女儿是杀人凶手，而她本人否认，你们会信天使长的话吗？绝不——在这神的世界上绝不，这一点你们自己清楚！倘若你们在心里对自己说：‘他认识苏珊·艾夫斯、爱她多年；他至今爱她，所以她必定如他所想的那样’——那么兰伯特先生的热忱雄辩就大功告成，我的冷峻逻辑就一败涂地了。

“所以我请诸位，用头脑，不要用心。请诸位狠狠打折的，不是兰伯特先生的诚挚、他的情谊、他的雄辩，而是他的判断力与轻信。老生常谈往往是最古老、最深刻的真理；其中最古老、最深刻的一条，便是那句格言：爱是盲目的。

“两场总体的交锋，我的职责所系，就应战到这里；至于条子、汽车与笑声这些更具体的，我到相应的位置再谈。泛泛之论到此为止，接下来是事实。”

===Original===

“These fall into two categories﻿—the first including the events leading up to and precipitating the crime, the second dealing with the execution of the crime itself.

“I propose to deal with them in their logical sequence. In the first category comes the prime factor in this case﻿—motive. Mr. Lambert has told you that that is the weakest factor in the state’s case; I tell you that it is the strongest. There has never come under my observation a more perfect example of an overwhelming motive springing from the very foundation of motivation﻿—from character itself.

“I want you to get this perfectly straight; it is of the most vital importance. There is never any convincing motive for murder, in that that implies an explanation that would seem plausible to the sane and well-balanced mind. There is something in any such mind that recoils in loathing and amazement that such a solution of any problem should seem possible. It makes no difference whether murder is committed﻿—as it has been committed﻿—for a million dollars or for five﻿—in revenge for a nagging word or for bestial cruelty﻿—for a quarrel over a pair of dice or over a pair of dark eyes﻿—to us it seems equally abhorrent, grotesque, and incredible. And so it is. But in some few cases we are able to study the deep springs in which this monster lurks, and this is one of them.

“I ask you to concentrate now on what you have learned as to the character of Susan Ives, from her own lips and from the lips of others﻿—the undisputed evidence that has been put before you. Forget for a moment that she is small and slight, sweet-voiced, clear-eyed﻿—a lady. Look within.

“From the time that we first see her, on the very threshold of girlhood, to the time that you have seen her with your own eyes here, she has shown a character that is perfectly consistent﻿—a character that is as resolute, as lawless, and as ruthless as you would find in any hardened criminal in this land. At the first touch of constraint or opposition she is metamorphosed into a dangerous machine, and woe to the one that stands in its way.

===Chinese===

“这些事实分为两类：第一类是酿成并触发罪行的一系列事件；第二类关乎罪行本身的实施。

“我打算依它们的逻辑顺序来讲。第一类里，头一件是本案的首要因素——动机。兰伯特先生告诉诸位，那是本州指控中最薄弱的一环；我告诉诸位，那是最坚实的一环。以我之见，从未有过比这更完美的范例：一个压倒一切的动机，径直发源于动机的根基——发源于品性本身。

“我要诸位把这一点弄得清清楚楚；它至关重要。谋杀从来不存在令人信服的动机——因为那意味着一个在心智健全、头脑平稳的人看来也属可信的解释。任何这样的头脑里都有一种东西，会又惊又憎地退缩：竟有人视此为解决问题之可能。为百万美元也罢，为五块美元也罢——为回敬一句唠叨也罢，为兽行的残虐也罢——为一掷骰子起争执也罢，为一双黑眼睛起争执也罢——谋杀在我们看来同样可憎、怪诞、不可置信。事实也确实如此。可在少数案件中，我们得以研究这怪物潜藏的深泉；本案便是其中之一。

“现在请诸位凝神于你们已获知的苏珊·艾夫斯的品性——从她自己的唇上，从他人的唇上——摆在你们面前那些无可争议的证词。暂且忘掉她娇小纤弱、声音甜美、目光清澈——一位淑女。向内看。

“从我们初次见到她——正当少女时代的门槛上——到你们在此亲眼见到她的今日，她展现出的品性一以贯之——这品性之决绝、之无法无天、之冷酷无情，与你在本地任何一个惯犯身上能找到的不相上下。一受约束或反对的触碰，她便化为一台危险的机器；挡在它路上的，遭殃。”

===Original===

“Seven years ago, over the bitter opposition of her adoring father, she decided to marry the man who had previously been Madeleine Bellamy’s lover, and who had, deservedly or undeservedly, somewhat of the reputation of the village scamp and ne’er-do-well. Her marriage to him broke her father’s heart. Shortly thereafter the old man died, and so bitter, relentless, and unforgiving is the heart of this daughter, whom he had longed to cherish and protect, that not once since she left it in pride and anger has she set foot within the boundaries of her childhood’s home.

“She returned, however, at the first opportunity to Rosemont; the arrogance that consumed her like a flame made it essential that she should be triumphantly reestablished on the grounds of her first defeat. And the triumph was a rich and intoxicating one. Wealthy, courted, admired, surrounded by a chorus of industrious flatterers, no wonder that she became obsessed with a sense of her power and importance. She was, in fact, undisputed queen of the little domain in which she lived, and her throne seemed far more secure than most.

“She was not precisely a benevolent monarch; poor little Kathleen Page and Melanie Cordier have testified to that, but then they had made the dangerous error of murmuring protests at the rule. A little judicious browbeating and starvation reduced them to the proper state of subjection, and all was well once more. Graciousness and generosity itself to all who bent the knee at the proper angle, as her mother-in-law and maid have testified, still, it required the merest flicker of insubordination to set the steel fingers twitching beneath the velvet glove.

“Nothing more than fugitive rebellions had penetrated this absolute monarchy, however, up to that bright summer afternoon when news reached its sovereign that there was an aspirant to the throne﻿—a powerful pretender﻿—an actual usurper, with the keys to the castle itself in her hand. The blood of Elizabeth of England, of Catherine of Russia, of Lucrezia of Italy rose in the veins of this other spoiled child to meet that challenge. And, gentlemen, we know too well the fate that befell those rash and lovely pretenders of old.

“Enough of metaphor. From the moment that Susan Ives knew that the beautiful daughter of the village dressmaker was trespassing on her property, Madeleine Bellamy was doomed.

===Chinese===

“七年前，她置痴心爱她的父亲的痛苦反对于不顾，决意嫁给出入过玛德琳·贝拉米情事的男人——此人无论冤枉与否，总背着几分村里浪荡子、败家子的名声。这桩婚姻伤透了父亲的心。不久老人便去世了；而这位他渴望珍爱庇护的女儿，心肠竟如此怨毒、如此狠绝、如此不肯饶恕——自她负气傲然离家，再没有踏上过童年故宅的门槛。

“然而一有机会她便回了罗斯蒙特；吞噬她如烈焰的傲慢，使她必须在初尝败绩之地凯旋般重新立定。而这凯旋确也丰饶醉人。富有、受追捧、受仰慕，身边围着一班勤快的谄媚者的合唱，难怪她渐渐迷醉于自己的权势与显要。事实上，她是自己那小小疆域里无可争议的女王，而且她的宝座看去远比多数王座安稳。

“她算不得一位仁厚的君主；可怜的小凯瑟琳·佩奇和梅拉妮·科迪尔已就此作证——不过她们也犯下了危险的错误：对统治低声抗议了几句。稍稍一番见机的威吓与克扣，便把她们规训到应有的臣服状态，于是一切复归安好。对一切以恰当角度屈膝的人，她自是宽厚慷慨之至——她的婆婆和女仆已就此作证——然而只需一丝不驯的火苗，天鹅绒手套下的钢指便抽动起来。

“不过，直至那个明亮的夏日午后，渗入这绝对君主制的，无非是几场转瞬即逝的小小叛乱；就在那天下午，消息传到君主耳中：有人觊觎起王位——一个强劲的王位觊觎者——一个真正的篡位者，手里攥着城堡本身的钥匙。英格兰的伊丽莎白、俄国的叶卡捷琳娜、意大利的卢克雷齐娅的血，在这个同样被宠坏的孩子的血管里涌起来，迎向那场挑战。而诸位先生，古往今来那些轻率而美丽的觊觎者落得什么下场，我们再清楚不过。

“比喻说够了。从苏珊·艾夫斯得知村里裁缝家的漂亮女儿擅入她的领地那一刻起，玛德琳·贝拉米就注定劫数难逃。”

===Original===

“So much for the motive. Now for the means. We will take Susan Ives’s own account of that evening﻿—the account that was finally wrung from her when she found, to her terror and despair, that the state had in its hands evidence absolutely damning and conclusive. The telephone call, Orsini’s vigil at the window, the tire tracks, the fingerprints﻿—all these successive blows brought successive changes in the fabric that the defendants were weaving for your benefit.

“It became evident early in the trial that their original tale of absolute innocence and ignorance would not bear inspection one minute, but they continued industriously to cut their cloth to fit our case until they were confronted with two or three little marks on the base of a lamp. Then and then only they saw the hopelessness of their plight, discarded the whole wretched, patched, tattered stuff, and tried frantically to replace it by a fabric bearing at least the outer pattern of candour. What candour under those circumstances is worth is for you to decide.

“Mr. Lambert assures you that they had both decided to stop short of perjury. If the conclusion of Stephen Bellamy’s first story on that stand was not in fact black perjury, whatever it may have been technically, is again for you to decide. I have little doubt of that decision.

“But in Mrs. Ives’s account of that evening’s doings, you have the outward and visible sign of truth, if not the inward and spiritual state. The story that she finally told you I believe to be substantially correct as far as outward events go﻿—up to the point where she entered the cottage door. From then on I believe it to be the sheerest fabrication. Let us follow it to that point.

“From the moment that Elliot Farwell informed her that Mimi Bellamy was carrying on an intrigue with her husband, her every act is a revelation. It is no pleasant task to inspect from then on the conduct of this loyal, gentle, generous and controlled spirit, but let us set ourselves to it. She has heard that her reign is threatened﻿—what does she do?

===Chinese===

“动机讲完了。接下来是手段。我们且采用苏珊·艾夫斯自己对那个傍晚的交代——那交代，是她惊恐绝望地发现本州手中已握有铁证如山、足以定谳的证据时，才终于被挤出来的。那通电话，奥西尼在窗边的守夜，轮胎印，指纹——这一记接一记的重击，使被告为诸位编织的那匹织物一改再改。

“庭审伊始便已分明：他们原先那套全然无辜、全然不知情的说法，一分钟也经不起推敲；可他们仍孜孜不倦地照着我们案情的尺寸裁布，直到灯座上那两三个小小印记摆到面前。直到那时，也唯有到那时，他们才看清处境之无望，把那整匹寒碜的、缝缝补补、破破烂烂的东西丢开，拼命换上一匹至少在外观纹样上带着坦白字样的织物。这种情形下的坦白值几个钱，由诸位来定夺。

“兰伯特先生向诸位保证，他们两人都早已决定绝不至于作伪证。斯蒂芬·贝拉米第一次在证人席上讲的那番话，其收梢若算不上不折不扣的伪证——姑无论法律技术上如何界定——同样由诸位定夺。我对那个裁断的结果，几乎没有疑问。

“但在艾夫斯太太对当晚行止的交代里，诸位得到了真相外在可见的表征，纵然未必是它内在的灵魂状态。她最终讲给诸位听的这个故事，我相信就外部事件而言大体属实——直到她跨进小屋门槛的那一点为止。从那以后，我相信它纯属捏造。我们就循着它走到那一点。

“从埃利奥特·法维尔告诉她咪咪·贝拉米与她丈夫私通的那一刻起，她的每一个举动都是一次自我暴露。从此检视这位忠贞、温柔、慷慨而克制的灵魂的行止，不是件惬意的差事，但让我们勉力为之。她听说自己的统治受到了威胁——她做了什么？”

===Original===

“She returns to her home, concealing the rage and terror working in her like a poison under a flow of laughter and chatter﻿—and cocktails. Susan Ives is a lawless individual, gentlemen﻿—the law was made for humbler spirits than hers. In her house, in this court, in that darkened cottage, she has shown you unhesitatingly her defiance and contempt of any law made by man﻿—and of one made by God.

“She is not as yet quite sure that Farwell has told her the truth; there is too much arrogance in her to believe that danger actually threatens her from that direction﻿—but, under the smiling mask, behind the clenched teeth, the poison is working. She goes to the hall to bid Farwell goodbye and to warn him not to give her knowledge of the intrigue away﻿—perhaps already a prophetic sense of her share in this dreadful business is formulating. And while she is speaking to him she sees in the mirror Melanie Cordier, placing the note in the book. It is the work of a minute to step into the study after Melanie has left, abstract the note, master the contents, and return to the living room, her guests, and Patrick. On the way back, she stopped in the hall long enough to eavesdrop and get her cue. With that cue as to the prospective poker game in her possession, her course was already clear. She went up to Patrick Ives with a lie on her lips and a blacker one in her heart, and told him that she was going to the movies that night with the Conroys.

“She then followed him again into the hall to spy on him while he counted the bonds; she followed him back to the study after dinner to spy on him again, to see where he put them; she got rid of him with a lie, broke into his desk, confirmed her worst suspicions, and decided definitely on a course of action. A telephone message to Stephen Bellamy, another lie from the foot of the stairs to her unsuspecting husband, and she was on her way.

===Chinese===

“她回到家中，把如毒药般在体内发作的狂怒与恐惧，掩在一川谈笑——和几杯鸡尾酒——之下。苏珊·艾夫斯是个无法无天的人，诸位——法律是为比她卑下的灵魂设立的。在自己家里，在这法庭上，在那间黑着灯的小屋里，她向诸位毫不迟疑地展示过她对任何人定之法——以及神定之法——的蔑视与轻侮。

“她此时还不能断定法维尔说的是实话；她的傲慢太多，不信危险竟真从那个方向逼来——可是，微笑的面具之下，咬紧的齿关后面，毒药正在发作。她走到门厅送法维尔出门，并警告他不得泄露她已知晓这桩私通——也许，她对自己在这桩可怕勾当里的份额，一种预言式的预感已在成形。就在她与法维尔说话的当口，她从镜子里看见梅拉妮·科迪尔把那张条子放进书里。梅拉妮前脚离开，她后脚便踅进书房，抽走条子，通晓内容，再回到客厅、宾客与帕特里克身边——这不过是一分钟的事。回来的路上，她在门厅驻足片刻，偷听到自己需要的话头。那场即将开局的扑克牌局既已在握，她的路线已然分明。她走到帕特里克·艾夫斯面前，唇上一个谎，心里一个更黑的谎，告诉他当晚要同康罗伊一家去看电影。

“随后她又跟到门厅，趁他清点债券时窥伺他；晚饭后她又跟回书房再次窥伺，看他把东西收在哪里；她用一个谎打发走他，撬开他的书桌，坐实了最坏的猜疑，就此定下行动方针。给斯蒂芬·贝拉米捎个电话，再在楼梯脚下对毫无疑心的丈夫撒一个谎——她便上了路。”

===Original===

“Before she reached the gate, something went wrong, and she returned to the house﻿—possibly for the reason that she gave you, possibly for another. At any rate, within a minute or so she was at her old task of eavesdropping and spying, and a minute or so later than that Patrick Ives was safely locked up, well out of the running when it came to protecting the foolish girl at the cottage or the maddened one on her way there. Susan Ives had successfully disposed of the greatest menace to the execution of her scheme. Perhaps fuel was added to the flame by what she heard from the room off the day nursery; perhaps she heard nothing at all and merely wanted to get Patrick out of the way. It is a matter of no great importance. She had accomplished her purpose and was on her way again, to meet Stephen Bellamy.

“It is the state’s contention that she went to that rendezvous with a knife in her pocket and murder in her heart. Patrick Ives has told you that the knife that the state put in evidence was not out of his possession that evening; it is for you to decide whether you believe him or not. But which knife struck the blow is of no great importance either. The knife that murdered Madeleine Bellamy was, as you have been told, a perfectly ordinary knife﻿—such a knife as might be found in any of your homes﻿—in the kitchen, in the pantry, in the tool chest. From any of these places Susan Ives might have procured one, cleansed it and replaced it. We need not let which one she actually procured give us great concern.

“Susan Ives herself has touched very briefly on that drive with Stephen Bellamy through the quiet, starlit summer night; she merely confirms Stephen Bellamy’s account, which is neither very coherent nor very convincing. The gist of it was that Sue Ives was occupied in proving Mimi’s guilt and he with denying it. Some such conversation may well have taken place.

===Chinese===

“还没到大门口，出了岔子，她回到宅里——缘故也许如她所讲，也许另有其故。总之一两分钟内，她已重操旧业，偷听窥伺；再一两分钟后，帕特里克·艾夫斯已被稳稳锁好，在保护小屋里那个傻姑娘、或拦住那个去小屋的疯女人的事上，彻底出局。苏珊·艾夫斯成功地除掉了她计划的最大威胁。兴许她从日间育儿室旁那间屋里听来的话给火焰添了柴；兴许她什么也没听见，只想把帕特里克支开。这无关宏旨。目的已达，她再度上路，去会斯蒂芬·贝拉米。

“本州的主张是：她赴那场约会，兜里揣着刀，心里揣着杀人。帕特里克·艾夫斯已告诉你们，本州列为证物的那把刀当晚没有离过他的手；信不信他，由诸位定夺。可究竟哪把刀行凶，同样无关宏旨。杀害玛德琳·贝拉米的那把刀，诸位已经听说了，是把再普通不过的刀——你们任何一家的宅子里都翻得着的刀——厨房里、配膳室里、工具箱里。随便哪一处，苏珊·艾夫斯都取得着一把，用净了再放回去。她究竟取的哪一把，不必叫我们大费心思。

“那一晚同斯蒂芬·贝拉米驱车穿过宁静星夜的行程，苏珊·艾夫斯本人只有寥寥数语；她只是印证了斯蒂芬·贝拉米的叙述——那叙述不算很连贯，也不算很可信。要旨无非：苏·艾夫斯忙着证明米米的罪，他忙着否认。这样一番对话，多半确实有过。”

===Original===

“The part that Stephen Bellamy played in the actual commission of this murder is a more enigmatic one than that of Susan Ives, if not less sinister. From the outset, it must have been perfectly clear to Mrs. Ives’s exceptionally shrewd mind that, if she did not want Stephen Bellamy at her heels as an avenging husband, she must lure him into the role of an accomplice. This, by means best known to herself, she accomplished. We have it on Stephen Bellamy’s own word that he entered that little room with her and left it with her, and we know that he sits beside her in this dock because they have elected to hang or go free together.

“Now as to what Mr. Lambert is pleased to refer to as their alibi, and then I have done.

“Of course, they have neither of them the shred of an alibi. Accepting the fact that they left the gas station shortly after nine and reached Stephen Bellamy’s at about ten, they would have had ample time to reach the Thorne place by the River Road, confront the waiting girl with the intercepted note, murder her, make good their escape, and return to Bellamy’s by ten o’clock. Later, Bellamy returns to the cottage alone to get the jewels, in order to give colour to the appearance of robbery and to remove any traces of the crime that they may have left behind them. Possibly it was then that he brought the lamp from the hall and smashed it at the dead girl’s feet. By then they had had time to work out a story in the remote possibility of their eventual discovery pretty thoroughly. At any rate, he took Susan Ives home and returned alone. I repeat, they have no alibi.

“ ‘Well, what of the laugh?’ you say. ‘What of the car that was not there?’ To which I echo, ‘What of them, indeed?’

“Gentlemen, just stop to think for one minute. Who heard that laugh? Who failed to see that automobile? Who fixed the hour for this murder at the moment that would come closest to establishing an alibi for these two? Why, the brother of Susan Ives﻿—the loving, the devoted, the adoring brother, who stood up here in this room and told you that he would do anything short of murder to protect his sister﻿—”

Lambert was on his feet, his eyes goggling in an ashen countenance. “He said nothing of the kind! Your Honour﻿—”

“He did not say that he would not commit murder?”

“He did not say that he would do anything short of it. Of all the﻿—”

===Chinese===

“在这桩谋杀的实际实施中，斯蒂芬·贝拉米扮演的角色，若不比苏珊·艾夫斯的更阴森，也更是个谜。打一开始，以艾夫斯太太那份过人的精明，必定看得一清二楚：若不愿斯蒂芬·贝拉米以复仇丈夫之姿紧随她脚后，就必须诱他入伙。这一层，她用只有她自己最清楚的手段办成了。我们听斯蒂芬·贝拉米亲口承认：他与她一同进了那间小屋，又与她一同离开；我们也知道，他此刻与她并肩坐在被告席上，因为他们选定要么一同上绞架，要么一同自由。

“至于兰伯特先生乐于称作他们不在场证明的东西——讲完这个，我就收尾。

“当然，他们两人谁也没有一丝不在场证明。承认他们九点刚过离开加油站、十点前后到达斯蒂芬·贝拉米家这个事实，他们有的是工夫沿河滨路抵达索恩庄园，拿截获的条子与那等候的姑娘对质，杀了她，从容脱身，十点前赶回贝拉米家。随后，贝拉米独自重返小屋取走珠宝，既给劫杀的外观上色，也抹去他们可能留下的罪痕。灯从门厅被他拿到那死去姑娘脚边砸掉，也许正是那一遭。到那时，万一东窗事发，一套说辞他们已有充分工夫想得周全。总之，他送苏珊·艾夫斯回家，独自又回去。我再说一遍：他们没有不在场证明。

“‘那那声笑怎么说？’你们问。‘那辆不在场的车怎么说？’我回敬一句：‘可不是怎么说？’

“诸位先生，且停下想一分钟。谁听见了那声笑？谁没有看见那辆汽车？是谁把凶案时刻钉得最贴近这两人的不在场证明？嘿，苏珊·艾夫斯的哥哥——那位慈爱的、深情的、溺爱的哥哥，就是他，站在这个房间里告诉你们，为了保护妹妹，除了杀人他什么都肯做——”

兰伯特霍然起立，灰白的脸上两眼暴凸。“他绝没有说过那种话！法官阁下——”

“他没有说过他不会杀人？”

“他没有说过除了杀人什么都肯做。这简直是——”

===Original===

“Then my memory is at fault,” remarked Mr. Farr blandly. “It was certainly my impression that such was the substance of his remarks. If it gives offense I withdraw it, and state simply that the person who has fixed the hour of the murder for you is Mrs. Patrick Ives’s brother, Mr. Douglas Thorne. There is not a shred of evidence save his as to the moment at which the murder took place﻿—not a shred. You are entirely at liberty to draw your own conclusions from that. If you decide that he was telling the absolute truth, I will concede even that possibility.

“Mr. Thorne simply tells you that at about nine-thirty on the evening of the nineteenth of June he heard a woman scream and a man laugh somewhere in the neighbourhood of the gardener’s cottage at Orchards. He adds that at the time he attached no particular importance to it, as he thought that it may have been young people skylarking in the neighbourhood﻿—and he may have been perfectly right. It no more establishes the hour of Madeleine Bellamy’s murder than it establishes the hour of the deluge.

“It is, in fact, perfectly possible that the murder took place after ten o’clock, after the visit to the Bellamy home and the alleged search along the road to the Conroys. Only one thing is certain: If it was nine-thirty when Mr. Thorne walked up those cottage steps, and if at that time there was no car in sight, then the hour of the murder was not nine-thirty. It may have been before that hour, it may have been after it. It was not then.

“So much for Mr. Lambert’s trump cards, the laugh and the car. There remains the theft of the note, which he claims Mrs. Ives had no interest in denying. Of course she had every interest in denying it. If she admitted that she had found the note, then she would be forced to admit to the jury that she knew positively that Mimi was waiting in the cottage, and that did not fit in with her story at all. So she simply denies that she took it. And there goes their last trump.

“Stripped of glamour, of emotion, of eloquence, it is the barest, the simplest, the most appallingly obvious of cases, you see. There is not one single link in the chain missing﻿—not one.

===Chinese===

“那是我记岔了，”法尔先生淡淡地说。“他的话在我印象里确实是大意如此。若此话冒犯，我收回，只简单陈述一点：替诸位钉定凶案时刻的，是帕特里克·艾夫斯太太的兄长，道格拉斯·索恩先生。凶案发生于哪一刻，除了他的证词，没有一星证据——没有一星。诸位完全有自由由此得出各自的结论。你们若裁定他讲的全然是实，连这个可能我也认。

“索恩先生不过告诉你们：六月十九日晚九点半上下，他在果园庄花匠小屋左近听见一个女人尖叫、一个男人大笑。他补充说，当时他未觉其重要，以为多半是年轻人在附近嬉闹——他多半也没想错。这既确立不了玛德琳·贝拉米遇害的钟点，也确立不了大洪水的钟点。

“事实上，凶案完全可能发生在十点以后，在造访贝拉米家和所谓沿路寻人之后。只有一件事是确定的：倘若索恩先生走上小屋台阶时是九点半，而那时周围没有车，那么凶案的时刻就不是九点半。可以在这之前，可以在这之后。总之不在那一刻。

“兰伯特先生的两张王牌，笑声与汽车，到此为止。剩下条子失窃一节——他声称艾夫斯太太否认此事毫无利害。她当然处处有利害。她若承认找到过条子，就得向陪审团承认她确切知道米米在小屋等候，而这对她的故事毫不对榫。于是她干脆否认拿了它。他们最后一张王牌，就此报销。

“剥掉光鲜、剥掉情感、剥掉雄辩，诸位看，这是最赤裸、最简单、最触目惊心地明白的一桩案子。链子上不缺一环——一环也不缺。”

===Original===

“Unless someone came to you here and said, ‘I saw the knife in Susan Ives’s hand, I saw it rise, I saw it fall, I heard the crash of that girl’s body and saw the white lace of her frock turn red’﻿—unless you heard that with your own ears, you could not have a clearer picture of what happened in that room. Not once in a thousand murder cases is there an eyewitness to the crime. Not once in five hundred is there forged so strong a chain of evidence as now lies before you.

“There was only one person in all the world to whom the death of Madeleine Bellamy was a vital, urgent, and imperative necessity. The woman to whom it was all of this﻿—and more, far more, since words are poor substitutes for passions﻿—has told you with her own lips that at ten o’clock on that night she stood over the body of that slain girl and saw her eyes wide in the dreadful and unseeing stare of death. When Susan Ives told you that, she told you the truth; and she told you the truth again when she said that when you knew that she had stood there, she did not believe that it would be possible for you to credit that the one fact had no connection with the other. Nor do I believe it, gentlemen﻿—nor do I believe it.

“By her side, in that room, stood Stephen Bellamy. By his own confession it was he who closed the eyes of that slain girl, he who touched her hand. By his own confession he has told you that he did not believe it possible that you would credit that he stood there at that time and yet had no knowledge of her death. Nor do I believe it, gentlemen﻿—nor do I believe it.

“Mr. Lambert has told you that to him has fallen the most solemn task that can fall to the lot of any man﻿—that of pleading for the gift of human life. There is a still more solemn task, I believe, and that task has fallen to me. I must ask you not for life but for death.

“The law does not exact the penalty of a life for a life in the spirit of vengeance or of malice. It asks it because the flame of human life is so sacred a thing that it is business of the law to see that no hand, however powerful, shall be blasphemously lifted to extinguish that flame. It is in order that your wives and daughters and sisters may sleep sweet and safe at night that I stand before you now and tell you that because they lifted that hand, the lives of Stephen Bellamy and Susan Ives are forfeit.

===Chinese===

“除非有人到庭对你们说：‘我看见刀在苏珊·艾夫斯手里，看它举起，看它落下，听见那姑娘身体砸地的声响，看见她白蕾丝的裙子转成红色’——除非你们亲耳听见这样的话，否则你们对那间屋里发生的事，已不可能有一幅更清楚的图景。千桩凶案里，难有一桩目睹犯罪的人证。五百桩里，难有一回锻出如你们面前这条一般坚硬的证据链。

“普天之下，只有一个人，玛德琳·贝拉米之死对她是性命交关、刻不容缓、势在必行的必需。这个女人——那件事之于她还不止如此，远远不止，因为言语之于激情向来贫弱——已亲口告诉你们：当晚十点，她站在那被害姑娘的遗体旁，看见她双目圆睁，是死亡那可怖而不见物的凝视。苏珊·艾夫斯告诉你们这一点时，讲的是实话；她再说一遍实话——她说她知道，一旦你们知道她在场，她便不信你们会相信这件事与那件事毫无关联。我也不信，诸位先生——我也不信。

“她身边，在那间屋里，站着斯蒂芬·贝拉米。照他自己的供认，是他合上那被害姑娘的眼睛，是他碰了她的手。照他自己的供认，他已告诉你们：他不信你们会相信，他彼时站在那里，却对她的死毫不知情。我也不信，诸位先生——我也不信。

“兰伯特先生告诉你们，落在他身上的，是人间最庄重的任务——为生命之赐恳求。还有一个更庄重的任务，我想，那个任务落在了我的身上。我必须向你们求的，不是生，是死。

“法律索取以命偿命，不是出于复仇或恶意的戾气。法律索取它，是因为人的生命之焰是那样神圣的东西，法律的职分便是确保没有任何一只手——无论多么有权势的手——得以亵渎地举起，扑灭那焰。正为了你们的妻子、女儿、姐妹能在夜里睡得香甜安稳，我才站在你们面前告诉你们：因为他们举起了那只手，斯蒂芬·贝拉米和苏珊·艾夫斯的性命，已经没收。”

===Original===

“These two believed that behind the bulwarks of power, of privilege, of wealth, and of position, they were safe. They were not safe; they have discovered that. And if those barriers can protect them now, if still behind them they can find shelter and security and a wall to shield them as they creep back to their ruined hearthstones, then I say to you that the majesty of the law is a mockery and the sacredness of human life is a mockery, and the death penalty in this great state is a mockery.

“There was never in this state a more wicked, brutal, and cold-blooded murder than that of Madeleine Bellamy. For Susan Ives and Stephen Bellamy, the two who now stand before you accused of that murder, I ask, with all solemnity and fully aware of the tragic duty that I impose on each one of you, the verdict of guilty of murder in the first degree. If you can find it in your hearts, in your souls, or your consciences to render any other verdict, you are more fortunate than I believe you to be.”

In the hushed silence that followed his voice, all eyes turned to the twelve who sat there unmoving, their drawn, pale faces, tired-eyed and tight-lipped, turned toward the merciless flame that burned behind the prosecutor’s white face.

The redheaded girl asked in a desolate small voice that sounded very far away, “Is it all over now? Are they going now?”

“No﻿—wait a moment; there’s the judge’s charge. Here, what’s Lambert doing?”

He was on his feet, swaying a little, his voice barely audible.

“Your Honour, a note has been handed to me this moment. It is written on the card of the principal of the Eastern High School, Mr. Randolph Phipps.”

“What are the contents of this note?”

Lambert settled his glasses on his nose with a shaken hand. “It says﻿—it says:

“My dear Mr. Lambert:

“Before this case goes to the jury, I consider it my duty to lay before them some knowledge of the most vital importance that is my possession, and that for personal reasons I have withheld up to the present time, in the hope that events would render it unnecessary for me to take the stand. Such has unfortunately not been the case, and I therefore put myself at your disposal. Will you tell me what my next step should be? The facts are such as make it imperative that I should be permitted to speak.

“Randolph Phipps.”

===Chinese===

“这两个人曾以为，凭着权势、特权、财富与地位的重垒壁垒，他们是安全的。他们并不安全；这一点他们已经发现了。倘若那些壁垒如今还能保住他们，倘若他们还能躲在其后寻得荫庇与安稳，有墙可倚，爬回自己坍塌的灶台，那我便对你们说：法律的威严是句空话，生命的神圣是句空话，这个伟大州的死刑也是句空话。

“本州从不曾有过比玛德琳·贝拉米案更邪恶、更残暴、更冷血的凶杀。对苏珊·艾夫斯和斯蒂芬·贝拉米——如今站在你们面前被控此罪的这两个人——我怀着全部的郑重，并充分明白我加诸你们每个人的悲惨职责，请求一级谋杀有罪的裁决。你们若能在心里、在灵魂里、在良知里作出别的裁决，那你们比我相信的更有福气。”

他的话音之后是一片屏息的寂静，所有的目光都转向端坐不动的十二个人；他们疲惫苍白、眼下带倦、嘴唇紧抿的脸，转向检察官白脸后面燃烧的那簇无情的火焰。

红头发姑娘用一个小小的、荒凉的、听着极远的声音问：“都完了吗？他们现在就要去了吗？”

“不——等一等；还有法官训示。咦，兰伯特这是干什么？”

他站在那儿，身子微微摇晃，嗓音细不可闻。

“法官阁下，方才有人递给我一张条子。写在东方中学校长兰道夫·菲普斯先生的名片上。”

“条子内容是什么？”

兰伯特用发颤的手把眼镜架上鼻梁。“上面说——它说：

“亲爱的兰伯特先生：

“在此案交给陪审团之前，我认为有责任向他们呈上我所掌握的一件至为紧要的知识；出于私人缘故，我一直隐忍到今天，指望事态发展会令我出庭成为不必要。不幸事与愿违，因此我听候差遣。可否请您示下我下一步该怎么做？这些事实，非容我开口不可。

“兰道夫·菲普斯。”

===Original===

Judge Carver said slowly, “May I see the note?” Lambert handed it up in those shaking fingers. “Thank you. A most extraordinary performance,” commented the judge dispassionately. After a moment he said more dispassionately still:

“The Court was about to adjourn in any case until tomorrow morning. It does not care to deliver its charge to the jury at this late hour of the day, and we will therefore convene again at ten tomorrow. In the meantime the Court will take the note under advisement. See that Mr. Phipps is present in the morning. Court is dismissed.”

“I don’t believe that I’ll be here in the morning,” said the redheaded girl in that same small monotone.

“Not be here?” The reporter’s voice was a howl of incredulity. “Not be here, you little idiot? Did you hear what Lambert read off that card?”

“I don’t think that I’ll live till morning,” said the redheaded girl.

The seventh day of the Bellamy trial was over.

===Chinese===

卡弗法官缓缓地说：“可以看看条子吗？”兰伯特用那双发颤的手呈了上去。“谢谢。真是非同寻常的一出，”法官不动声色地评道。片刻后他说得更不动声色了：

“无论如何本庭正要休庭至明晨。本庭不愿在今日这样晚的钟点向陪审团作训示，故明晨十点再行开庭。其间，本庭将对条子加以斟酌。务必让菲普斯先生明晨到庭。退庭。”

“我看我明早到不了这儿了，”红头发姑娘用同样小小的、平平板板的声音说。

“到不了？”记者的嗓音成了一声难以置信的嚎。“到不了，你这小傻瓜？兰伯特念的那张名片你听见了没有？”

“我看我活不到早晨了，”红头发姑娘说。

贝拉米审判的第七天，结束了。

## VIII / 八
===Original===

The redheaded girl had not realized how tired she was until she heard Ben Potts’s voice. He stood there as straight as ever, but where were the clear bugle tones that summoned the good burghers of Redfield morning after morning? A faint, a lamentable, echo of his impressive “Hear ye! Hear ye!” rang out feebly, and the redheaded girl slumped back dispiritedly in her chair, consumed with fatigue as with a fever.

“Sleep well?” inquired the reporter with amiable anxiety.

The redheaded girl turned on him eyes heavy with scorn. “Sleep?” she repeated acidly. “What’s that?”

Judge Carver looked as weary as Ben Potts sounded, and the indefatigable Mr. Farr looked blanched and bitten to the bone with something deeper than fatigue. Only Mr. Lambert looked haler and heartier than he had for several interminable days; and the faces of Stephen Bellamy and Susan Ives were as pale, as controlled, and as tranquil as ever.

Judge Carver let his gavel fall heavily. “The Court has given careful consideration as to the advisability of admitting the evidence in question last night, and has decided that it may be admitted. Mr. Lambert!” Mr. Lambert bounded joyfully forward. “Is the Court correct in understanding that Mr. Phipps is your witness?”

“Quite correct, Your Honour.”

“Let him be called.”

“Mr. Randolph Phipps!”

The principal of Eastern High School was a tall man; there was dignity in the way he held his head and moved his long, loose limbs, but all the dignity in the world could not still the nervous tremor of his hands or school the too sensitive mouth to rigidity. Under straight, heavy brows, the eyes of a dreamer startled from deep sleep looked out in amazement at a strange world; the sweep of dark hair above the wide brow came perilously close to being Byronic; only the height of his cheek bones and the width of his mouth saved him from suggesting a matinée idol of some previous era. He might have been thirty-five, or forty, or forty-five. His eyes were eighteen.

“Mr. Phipps, it is the understanding of this court that you have a communication to make of peculiar importance. You understand that in making that statement you will, of course, be subject to the usual course of direct and cross-examination?”

“I understand that﻿—yes.”

“Very well. You may proceed with the examination, Mr. Lambert.”

“Mr. Phipps, where were you on the night of the nineteenth of June?”

===Chinese===

红头发姑娘直到听见本·波茨的声音，才意识到自己有多累。他还像往常一样笔挺地站着，可那每天清晨唤起雷德菲尔德良民百姓的嘹亮号角般的嗓音哪儿去了？他那声威风凛凛的“开庭！开庭！”如今只剩一缕微弱得可怜的回响；红头发姑娘颓然瘫回椅子里，疲倦如热病般把她烧穿。

“睡得好吗？”记者带着和气的关切问。

红头发姑娘朝他转过一双盛满轻蔑的眼睛。“睡觉？”她刻薄地重复。“那是什么？”

卡弗法官看上去和本·波茨的嗓音听上去一样倦怠；不知疲倦的法尔先生则面有菜色，被一种比疲劳更深的东西啃到了骨头。唯独兰伯特先生，比那几个没完没了的日子里的任何时候都更硬朗、更精神；斯蒂芬·贝拉米和苏珊·艾夫斯的脸，则一如既往地苍白、克持、平静。

卡弗法官沉沉落下法槌。“关于昨夜提及的那项证据可否采纳，本庭已仔细斟酌，现裁定：可以采纳。兰伯特先生！”兰伯特先生欢天喜地抢步上前。“本庭理解无误的话，皮普斯先生是你的证人？”

“完全正确，庭上。”

“传他到庭。”

“伦道夫·皮普斯先生！”

东部中学的校长是个高个子男人；他昂头移动那副修长松弛的四肢，自有威仪，可全世界的威仪也按不住他双手紧张的颤抖，也管不住那张过于敏感的嘴让它僵硬。平直浓重的眉毛下，一双从沉睡中惊醒的做梦人的眼睛，惊异地打量着一个陌生的世界；宽阔前额上那一绺后掠的黑发，离“拜伦式”只差一线；多亏颧骨的高度和嘴巴的宽度，才没让他像一个过气时代的日场偶像。他也许三十五，也许四十，也许四十五。他的眼睛十八岁。

“皮普斯先生，本庭了解，您有一件特别重要的事要陈述。您可明白，作此陈述之后，您自然要照常接受直接询问与盘问？”

“我明白——是的。”

“很好。你可以开始询问了，兰伯特先生。”

“皮普斯先生，六月十九日晚上，您在哪里？”

===Original===

“On the night of the nineteenth of June,” said Mr. Phipps, in the clear, carrying voice of one not unaccustomed to public speaking, “I spent about three hours on the Thorne estate at Orchards. Some things occurred during that time that I feel it my duty to make known to the jury in this case.”

“What were you doing on the Thorne place?”

“I suppose that I was doing what is technically known as trespassing. It did not occur to me at the time that it was a very serious offense, as I knew the place to be uninhabited﻿—still, I suppose that I was perfectly aware that I had no business there.”

“You had no especial purpose in going there?”

“Oh, yes; I went there because I had selected it as a pleasant place for a picnic supper.”

“You were alone?”

“No﻿—no, I was not alone.” Mr. Phipps suddenly looked forty-five and very tired.

“Other people were accompanying you on this﻿—this excursion?”

“One other person.”

“Who was this other person?”

“A friend of mine﻿—a young lady.”

“What was the name of this young woman?”

“Is it necessary to give her name? I hope﻿—I hope with all my heart﻿—that that will not be necessary.” The low, urgent, unhappy voice stumbled in its intensity. “My companion was quite a young girl. We both realize now that we committed a grave indiscretion, but I shall never forgive myself if my criminal stupidity has involved her.”

“I am afraid that we shall have to have her name.”

“I am a married man,” said Mr. Phipps, in a clear voice that did not stumble. “I am placing this information before the Court at no small sacrifice to myself. It seems to me to place too heavy a penalty on my decision to come forward at this moment if you ask me to involve another by so doing. The girl who was with me that evening was one of my pupils; she is at present engaged to a young man to whom she is entirely devoted; publicity of the type that this means is in every way abhorrent to her. I request most urgently that she shall not be exposed to it.”

“Mr. Phipps,” said Judge Carver gravely, “you have been permitted to take the stand at your own request. It is highly desirable that any information, of the importance that you have implied that in your possession to be, should be as fully corroborated as possible. It is therefore essential that we should have the name of this young woman.”

“Her name is Sally Dunne,” said Mr. Phipps.

===Chinese===

“六月十九日晚上，”皮普斯先生说，那嗓音清朗致远，出自一个并非不惯于公众演讲的人，“我在奥查兹的索恩庄园度过了大约三个小时。其间发生的一些事情，我认为有责任让本案陪审团知道。”

“您在索恩庄园做什么？”

“我想，我干的是法律上所谓‘擅入’。当时我并不觉得那是什么重罪，因为我知道那地方无人居住——不过，我想我完全清楚自己没有权利待在那里。”

“您去那里没有什么特别目的？”

“哦，有；我去那里，是因为我选了它当野餐晚饭的好地方。”

“您独自一人？”

“不——不，我不是一个人。”皮普斯先生看上去忽然四十五岁，而且疲惫不堪。

“还有旁人陪您进行这次——这次郊游？”

“还有一个人。”

“那人是谁？”

“我的一位朋友——一位年轻女士。”

“这位年轻女士叫什么名字？”

“一定要说出她的名字吗？我希望——我由衷希望——不必如此。”低沉、急切、痛苦的嗓音在激切中磕绊。“我的同伴是个非常年轻的姑娘。如今我们都明白，我们犯下了严重的不检点，可倘若我的罪责般的愚蠢连累了她，我永远不会原谅自己。”

“恐怕我们必须知道她的名字。”

“我是个有妇之夫。”皮普斯先生说，嗓音清朗，不再磕绊。“我把这些信息呈交本庭，自己也付出了不小的牺牲。而如今你们又要我在这样做的同时牵连另一个人，在我看来，这对我在此刻挺身而出的决定，是过于沉重的惩罚。那晚同我在一起的姑娘是我的学生；她目前已与一位她全心投入的年轻人订婚；此事所意味的这种曝光，对她而言是彻头彻尾的憎厌。我极其恳切地请求，不要让她承受这个。”

“皮普斯先生，”卡弗法官庄重地说，“您是应自己的请求获准出庭的。凡属您所暗示的这种重要性的信息，都极有必要尽可能充分地获得佐证。因此，我们必须知道这位年轻女士的名字。”

“她叫萨莉·邓恩。”皮普斯先生说。

===Original===

“Is she also prepared to take the stand?”

“She is prepared to do whatever is essential to prevent a miscarriage of justice. She is naturally extremely reluctant to take the stand.”

“Is she in court?”

“She is.”

“Miss Dunne will be good enough not to leave the courtroom without the Court’s permission. You may proceed, Mr. Phipps.”

“We arrived at Orchards at a little after eight,” said Mr. Phipps. “Miss Dunne took the half-past-seven bus from Rosemont, left it a short distance beyond Orchards, and walked back to the spot where I had arranged to meet her, just inside the gate. We did not arrive together, as I was apprehensive that it might cause a certain amount of gossip if we were seen together.”

“How had you come to choose Orchards, Mr. Phipps?”

“Miss Dunne had on several occasions commented on the beauty of the place and expressed a desire to see it more thoroughly, and it was in order to gratify that desire that the party was planned. As I say, we met at the gate and walked on up the drive past the lodge and the little driveway that leads to the gardener’s cottage to a small summerhouse, about five hundred feet beyond the cottage itself. It contained a little furniture﻿—a table and some chairs and benches﻿—and it was there that we decided to have our supper. Miss Dunne had brought a luncheon box with her containing fruit and sandwiches, and we spread it on the table and began to eat. Neither of us was particularly hungry, however, and we decided to keep what remained of the food﻿—about half the contents of the box, I think﻿—in case we wanted it later, and to do some reading before it got too dark to see. I had brought with me the *Idylls of the King*, with the intention of reading it aloud.”

“The book is of no importance, Mr. Phipps.”

“No,” said Mr. Phipps, in a tone of slight surprise. “No, I suppose not. You are probably quite right. Well, in any case, we read for quite a while, until it began to get too dark to see, and after that we sat there conversing.”

The fluent voice with its slightly meticulous pronunciation paused, and Lambert moved impatiently. “And then, Mr. Phipps?”

===Chinese===

“她也准备出庭作证吗？”

“为防止正义蒙冤，凡属必需，她都准备去做。出庭作证，她自然是极其不情愿的。”

“她在法庭上吗？”

“在。”

“请邓恩小姐务必不经本庭许可不得离开法庭。请继续，皮普斯先生。”

“我们八点刚过到达奥查兹。”皮普斯先生说。“邓恩小姐从罗斯蒙特乘七点半的公共汽车，在奥查兹过去不远下车，走回我约好等她的地方，就在大门里面。我们没有一起到达，因为我担心被人撞见我们同行会惹起一些闲话。”

“您怎么会选中奥查兹的，皮普斯先生？”

“邓恩小姐曾多次赞叹那地方的景致，说想好好看看，此行正是为了遂她的愿。如我所说，我们在大门口会合，沿车道往上走，经过门房和通往园丁小屋的那条小岔道，来到一座小凉亭，离小屋本身约五百英尺。亭里有几件家具——一张桌子、几把椅子几条长凳——我们便决定就在那儿用晚饭。邓恩小姐带了一只午餐盒，装着水果和三明治，我们摊开在桌上吃了起来。不过我们俩都不怎么饿，便决定把剩下的食物——我想大约半盒——留着以防待会儿想吃，趁着天色还没黑到看不见，先读点书。我带了《国王之歌》，打算朗读。”

“那本书无关紧要，皮普斯先生。”

“哦。”皮普斯先生略带意外地说。“哦，想必是。你们多半说得对。总之，我们读了相当一阵子，直到天黑得看不见字，之后便坐在那儿谈话。”

那流利的、发音一丝不苟的嗓音停住了，兰伯特不耐烦地动了一下。“然后呢，皮普斯先生？”

===Original===

“Yes. I was trying to recollect precisely what it was that caused us to move from the summerhouse. I think that it was Miss Dunne who suggested that it was rather close and stuffy there, because of the fact that the structure was smothered in vines; she asked if there wasn’t somewhere cooler that we could go to sit. I said: ‘There’s the gardener’s cottage. We might try the veranda there.’ You could just see the roof of it through the trees. I pointed it out to her, and we started﻿—”

“You were familiar with the layout of the estate?”

“Oh, quite. That was one of the principal reasons why we had gone there. I had once done some tutoring in Latin and physics with Mr. Thorne’s younger son Charles﻿—the one who was killed in the war. We had been in the habit of using the summerhouse, which was his old playhouse, as a schoolroom.”

“That was some time ago?”

“About fifteen years ago﻿—sixteen perhaps. I had just graduated from college myself, and Charles Thorne was going to Princeton that fall.”

“But you still remembered your way about?”

“Oh, perfectly. I was about to say that we did not approach it from the main drive, but cut across the lawns, pushed through the shrubbery at the back and came up to it from the rear. We had just reached the little dirt drive back of the cottage, and were perhaps a hundred feet away from the house itself, when we heard voices, and Miss Dunne exclaimed: ‘There’s someone in the cottage. Look, the side window is lighted.’ I was considerably startled, as I had made inquiries about the gardener and knew that he was in Italy.

“I stood still for a moment, debating what to do next, when one of the voices in the cottage was suddenly raised, and a woman said quite clearly, ‘You wouldn’t dare to touch me﻿—you wouldn’t dare!’ Someone laughed and there was a little scuffling sound, and a second or so after that a scream﻿—a short, sharp scream﻿—and the sound of something falling with quite a clatter, as though a chair or a table had been overturned.

“I was in rather a nervous and overwrought state of mind myself that evening, and before I thought what I was doing I laughed, quite loudly. Miss Dunne whispered, ‘Be careful! They’ll hear you.’ Just as she spoke, the light went out in the cottage and I said, ‘Well, Sally, evidently we aren’t the only indiscreet people around here this evening. I’d better get you out of this.’

===Chinese===

“哦。我正想精确回忆，究竟是什么促使我们离开凉亭。我记得是邓恩小姐提议，说那里又闷又热，因为整座亭子被藤蔓裹得严严实实；她问有没有凉快些的地方可坐。我说：‘有园丁小屋。咱们可以去试试那儿的游廊。’隔着树梢刚能望见小屋的屋顶。我指给她看，我们便动身——”

“您熟悉庄园的布局？”

“哦，相当熟悉。这正是我们去那里的主要原因之一。我曾给索恩先生的小儿子查尔斯补习过拉丁文和物理——就是阵亡在大战里的那位。我们惯常在那座凉亭里上课，那原是他儿时的游戏屋。”

“那是相当久以前了？”

“大约十五年前——也许十六年。我自己刚出大学门，查尔斯·索恩那年秋天要去普林斯顿。”

“可您还记得路？”

“哦，记得清清楚楚。我正要说，我们没有从主车道过去，而是抄草坪斜穿，从后面拨开灌木丛，从小屋背后走近。我们刚到小屋后面那条土岔道，离屋子本身约莫一百英尺，就听见了人声，邓恩小姐失声道：‘小屋里有人。瞧，侧窗亮着灯。’我吃了一惊，因为我打听过那园丁，知道他在意大利。

“我呆立了片刻，盘算下一步怎么办，这时小屋里一个人声陡然拔高，一个女人清清楚楚地说：‘你不敢碰我——你不敢！’有人笑了，响起一小阵扭打似的动静，一两秒之后是一声尖叫——短促、尖利的一声尖叫——接着是什么东西哗啦倒下，像是椅子或桌子翻了。

“我那晚本就心神紧张、过度亢奋，还没想明白自己在干什么，就笑出了声，还相当响。邓恩小姐悄声说：‘小心！他们会听见你的。’话音未落，小屋的灯灭了，我说：‘好吧，萨莉，看样子今晚这一带不检点的不止我们俩。我还是带你离开这是非之地吧。’

===Original===

“Just as I was speaking I heard steps on the main driveway and the sound of someone whistling. The whistling kept coming closer every second, and I whispered, ‘Someone’s coming in here. We’d better stand back in those bushes by the house.’ There were some very tall lilacs at the side of the house under the windows, and we tiptoed over and pushed back into them. After a minute or so, we heard someone go up the steps, and then a bell rang inside the house. There wasn’t any sound at all for a minute; then we could hear the steps coming down the porch stairs again, and a moment later heard them on the gravel, and a moment later still they had died away.

“I said, ‘That was a close call﻿—too many people around here entirely. Let’s make it two less.’ We tiptoed out past the cottage to the main road and started back toward the lodge gates, walking along the grass beside the road in order not to make any noise. We were almost back to the gates when Miss Dunne stopped me.”

“Do you know what time it was, Mr. Phipps?”

“I am not sure of the time. I looked at my watch last when it began to get too dark to read﻿—shortly before nine. We did not start for the cottage until a few minutes later, and it is my impression that it must have been between quarter to ten and ten. We had been walking very slowly, but even at that pace it should not take more than twenty minutes.”

“It was dark then?”

“Oh, yes; it had been quite dark for some time, though it was possible to distinguish the outline of objects. It was a very beautiful starlight night.”

“Quite so. What caused Miss Dunne to stop you?”

“She exclaimed suddenly, ‘Oh, good heavens, I haven’t got my lunch box! I must have left it in the bushes by the cottage.’ I said, ‘Perhaps you left it in the summerhouse,’ but she was quite sure that she hadn’t, as she remembered distinctly thinking just before we reached the cottage that it was a nuisance lugging it about. She was very much worried, as it had her initial stenciled on it in rather a distinctive way, and she was afraid that someone that she knew might possibly find it and recognize it, and that if they returned it, her parents might learn that she had been at Orchards that night.”

“Her parents were not aware of this expedition?”

“They were not, sir. They had both gone to New Hampshire to attend the funeral of Mr. Dunne’s mother.”

“Proceed, Mr. Phipps.”

===Chinese===

“我正说着，就听见主车道上有脚步声，还有谁在吹口哨。口哨声一秒比一秒近，我悄声道：‘有人进来了。咱们最好退到屋子旁边那些灌木里去。’屋侧窗下有几丛很高的丁香，我们踮脚过去，挤进了树丛。约一分钟后，听见有人走上台阶，接着屋里的铃响了。有一分钟完全没有声息；随后脚步又下了游廊台阶，片刻后踩上碎石路，再片刻，便消逝了。

“我说：‘好险——这一带的人实在太多了。咱们俩就先撤吧。’我们踮着脚绕过小屋回到主路，朝门房大门往回走，为了不出声响，专走路边的草带。快到大门口时，邓恩小姐拦住了我。”

“您知道那是什么时候吗，皮普斯先生？”

“时间我拿不准。我最后一次看表，是天黑得没法读书的时候——九点差一点。几分钟后我们才动身去小屋，我印象里那必然是九点三刻到十点之间。我们走得很慢，可即便按那个速度，也用不了二十分钟。”

“那时天黑了？”

“哦，是的；已经黑了一阵子了，不过还辨得出物体的轮廓。那是个星光极美的夜晚。”

“正是。邓恩小姐为什么拦住您？”

“她突然叫道：‘哦，天哪，我的午餐盒不见了！准是落在小屋旁的灌木丛里了。’我说：‘兴许落在凉亭了。’可她确信没有，因为她清楚记得，快到小屋时还琢廑过，拎着它真是个累赘。她急得不行，因为盒子上用一种相当显眼的样式喷着她名字的首字母，她怕被熟人捡到认出来，万一人送回去，她父母就会知道她那晚去过奥查兹。”

“她的父母不知道这次出行？”

“不知道，先生。他们双双去新罕布什尔，参加邓恩先生母亲的葬礼去了。”

“请继续，皮普斯先生。”

===Original===

“Miss Dunne seemed so upset over the loss of the box that I finally agreed to go back with her to look for it, though there seemed to me a very slight chance of anyone identifying it, and I did not particularly care to risk arousing anyone who still might be in the cottage. I had a flashlight, however, and we decided to make a hurried search as quietly as possible; so we started back, retracing our steps and keeping a sharp lookout for the box.

“When we got to the dirt cutoff leading to the cottage from the main driveway, we took it and approached as quietly as possible, standing for a moment just at the foot of the steps where the lilac bushes began and listening to see whether we could hear anything within. Miss Dunne said, ‘There’s not a sound, and no light either. I don’t believe there’s a soul around.’

“I said, ‘Someone has closed the windows and pulled down the shades in this front room. It was open when we were here before.’ Sally said, ‘Well, never mind﻿—let’s look quickly and get away from here. I think it’s a horrid place.’ I turned on the flashlight and said, ‘We were much farther back than this.’ She said, ‘Yes; we were beyond these windows. Look! what’s this?’

“Something was glittering in the grass at the side of the steps, and I bent down and picked it up. It was a small object of silver and black enamel. I turned the light on it, and Miss Dunne said, ‘It’s one of those cigarette lighters. Look, there is something written on it. It says, “Elliot from Mimi, Christmas.” ’

“Just then I heard a sound that made me look up. I said, ‘Listen, that’s a car.’ And I no more than had the words out of my mouth when I saw its headlights coming around the corner of the cutoff. I whispered, ‘Stand still﻿—don’t move!’ because I could see that the headlights wouldn’t catch us, as we were standing far back from the road; but Miss Dunne had already pushed back into the shrubbery about the house. I stood stock-still, staring at the car, which had drawn up at the steps. It was a small car﻿—a runabout, I think you call it﻿—”

“Could you identify the make, Mr. Phipps?”

===Chinese===

“邓恩小姐为丢盒子急成那样，我终于同意陪她回去找，尽管依我看被人认出的机会微乎其微，而我也不大情愿冒风险去惊动小屋里可能还在的人。不过我带着手电，我们决定尽快悄悄找一遍；于是折返回去，循原路走，一路留意那只盒子。

“走到主车道通往小屋的那条土岔道口，我们拐了进去，尽可能轻地靠近，在丁香丛起始的台阶脚下站了片刻，听屋里有没有动静。邓恩小姐说：‘一点声音也没有，灯也不亮。我看周围一个人也没有。’

“我说：‘有人关了这间前屋的窗，放下了帘子。咱们先前在这儿时，窗是开的。’萨莉说：‘唉，别管了——快找快走。我觉得这地方瘆人。’我打开手电，说：‘咱们那会儿站得比这靠后得多。’她说：‘对；在这几扇窗后面。瞧！这是什么？’

“台阶旁的草丛里有什么东西闪着光，我弯腰拾起来。那是个银质黑珐琅的小物件。我把电筒照上去，邓恩小姐说：‘是那种打火机。瞧，上面还刻着字呢。写的是“埃利奥特存念，米米赠，圣诞”。’

“正在这时，我听见一个声音，不由得抬起头。我说：‘听，是汽车。’话音未落，就看见车灯拐过岔道口。我悄声说：‘站着别动——千万别动！’因为我看得出车灯照不到我们，我们站得离路很远；可邓恩小姐已经退进了屋旁的灌木丛。我一动不动地站着，盯着那辆在台阶前停下的车。那是辆小车——你们大概管它叫单排座敞篷车——”

“您认得出是什么牌子吗，皮普斯先生？”

===Original===

“No, sir; I am not familiar with automobiles. Just a small dark, ordinary-looking car. Two people got out of it﻿—a man and a woman. They stood there for a moment on the steps, and when I saw who they were I came very close to letting out an exclamation of amazement. They went up the steps toward the front door.”

“Were they conversing?”

“Yes, but in low voices. I couldn’t hear anything until he said quite clearly, ‘No, it’s open﻿—that’s queer.’ They went in, and I whispered to Miss Dunne, ‘Do you know who that was? That was Stephen Bellamy, with Mrs. Patrick Ives.’ Just as I spoke I saw a light go on in the hall, and a second or so later it disappeared and one sprang up behind the parlour shades. I was just starting over toward Miss Dunne when there was a crash from the parlour﻿—a metallic kind of a crash, like breaking glass, and the light went out. I whispered, ‘Come on Sally; I’m going to get out of this!’ She started to come toward me, and someone inside screamed﻿—a most appalling sound, as though the person were in mortal terror. I assure you that it froze me to the spot, though it was only the briefest interval before I again heard voices on the porch.”

“Could you see the speakers, Mr. Phipps?”

“No; not until they were getting into the car. I was at this time standing just around the corner of the house, and so could not see the porch.”

“Could you distinguish what they were saying?”

“Not at first; they were both speaking together, and it was very confusing. It wasn’t until they appeared again in the circle of the automobile lights that I actually distinguished anything more than a few fragmentary words. Mr. Bellamy had his hand on Mrs. Ives’s wrist and he was saying﻿—”

Mr. Farr was on his feet, but much of the tiger had gone out of his spring. “Does the Court hold that what this witness claims that he heard one person say to another person is admissible evidence?”

“Of course it is admissible evidence!” Lambert’s voice was frantic with anxiety. “Words spoken on the scene of the crime, within a few minutes of the crime﻿—What about the rule of res gestae?”

Mr. Farr made an unpleasant little noise. “A few minutes? That’s what you call three quarters of an hour? When ejaculations made within two minutes have been ruled out after res gestae has been invoked?”

“It has been interpreted to admit whole sentences at a much﻿—”

===Chinese===

“认不出，先生；我不懂汽车。就是一辆深色的小车，样子寻常。车上下来两个人——一男一女。他们在台阶前站了片刻，等我看清他们是谁，差一点惊呼出声。他们拾级而上，朝前门走去。”

“他们在交谈吗？”

“在交谈，但声音很低。我一直什么也听不清，直到他清清楚楚地说：‘不，门开着——怪了。’他们进了屋，我悄声对邓恩小姐说：‘你知道刚才是谁吗？是斯蒂芬·贝拉米，和帕特里克·艾夫斯太太。’话音未落，我看见门厅亮起一盏灯，一两秒后又灭了，客厅窗帘后腾起另一盏。我刚要朝邓恩小姐那边走，客厅里传来一声爆响——一种金属质地的碎裂声，像玻璃碎了，灯随即熄灭。我悄声道：‘快，萨莉；我要带你离开这儿！’她刚朝我走来，屋里有人尖叫起来——那声音骇人至极，仿佛此人正陷于性命攸关的恐怖。我向您保证，那叫声把我钉在了原地，虽然只是短短一瞬，游廊上便又响起了人声。”

“您看得见说话的人吗，皮普斯先生？”

“看不见；直到他们上车才看见。我当时正站在屋角拐过去的地方，看不到游廊。”

“您分辨得出他们在说什么吗？”

“起初分辨不出；两人一起说话，乱得很。直到他们重新出现在汽车灯光的光圈里，我才真正听清了不止零星几个字。贝拉米先生的手抓着艾夫斯太太的手腕，正说——”

法尔先生站了起来，可那一扑里的虎威已去了大半。“本庭是否认为，这位证人声称听到的一个人对另一个人说的话，属于可采纳的证据？”

“当然是可采纳的证据！”兰伯特的嗓音急得发狂。“在罪案现场、案发几分钟之内说出的话——‘事实伴随之言’规则怎么算？”

法尔先生发出一个不悦的小声音。“几分钟？这就是你说的三刻钟？两分钟内脱口的话，都有过在援引事实伴随规则后被裁定排除的先例。”

“该规则的解释历来容许采纳远——”

===Original===

“Gentlemen”﻿—Judge Carver’s gavel fell with an imperious crash﻿—“you will be good enough to address the Court. Am I correct in understanding that what you desire is a ruling on the admissibility of this evidence, Mr. Farr?”

“That is all that I have requested, Your Honour.”

“Very well. In view of the gravity of this situation and the very unusual character of the testimony, the Court desires to show as great a latitude as possible in respect to this evidence. It therefore rules that it may be admitted. Is there any objection?”

“No objection,” said Mr. Farr, with commendable promptness, rallying a voice that sounded curiously flat. “It has been the object﻿—and the sole object﻿—of the state throughout this case to get at the truth. It is entirely willing to waive technicalities wherever possible in order that that end may be obtained.﻿ ﻿… No objection.”

“You may proceed, Mr. Phipps.”

“Mr. Bellamy was saying, ‘It makes no difference how innocent we are. If it were ever known that we were in that room tonight, you couldn’t get one person in the world to believe that we weren’t guilty, much less twelve. I’ve got to get you home. Get into the car.’ And they got into the car and drove off.”

“And then, Mr. Phipps?”

“And then, sir, I said to Miss Dunne, ‘Sally, that sounds like the voice of prophecy to me. If no one would believe that they were innocent, no one would believe that we are. Never mind the lunch box; I’m going to get you home too.’ ”

“You were aware that a murder had been committed?”

“A murder? Oh, not for one moment!” The quiet voice was suddenly vehement in its protest. “Not for one single moment! I thought simply that for some inexplicable reason Mr. Bellamy and Mrs. Ives had been almost suicidally indiscreet and had fortunately become aware of it at the last moment. It brought my own most culpable indiscretion all too vividly home to me, and I therefore proceeded to escort Miss Dunne back to her home, where I left her.”

“Yes﻿—exactly. Now, Mr. Phipps, just one or two questions more. On your first visit to the cottage, when you heard the woman’s voice cry, ‘Don’t dare to touch me,’ both the front and the rear of the cottage were under your observation, were they not?”

“At different times﻿—yes.”

===Chinese===

“二位先生，”卡弗法官的法槌威严地轰然落下，“请务必面向本庭陈述。我理解得对吗，法尔先生，你要的是本庭就该项证据的可采纳性作出裁定？”

“这正是我所请求的全部，庭上。”

“很好。鉴于局势之重大与证词性质之罕见，本庭愿就此项证据展现最大限度的宽容。故裁定：可以采纳。有无异议？”

“无异议。”法尔先生说，回应之快可嘉，重整出一副听着出奇平板的嗓音。“查究真相，一直是——也仅仅是——州方在本案中一贯的目标。为达此目的，只要可能，州方完全愿意放弃程序枝节。……无异议。”

“请继续，皮普斯先生。”

“贝拉米先生当时在说：‘咱们再清白也没用。只要让人知道咱们今晚进过那个房间，全世界你找不出一个人肯相信咱们无罪，更别说十二个了。我必须送你回家。上车。’于是他们上了车，开走了。”

“然后呢，皮普斯先生？”

“然后，先生，我对邓恩小姐说：‘萨莉，在我听来，这话像预言。要是没人肯信他们清白，也就没人会信咱们清白。别管午餐盒了；我也要送你回家。’”

“您当时知道已经发生了谋杀？”

“谋杀？哦，一刻也没有！”那安静的嗓音忽然激烈地抗辩。“一刹那也没有！我只是想，出于某种无法解释的原因，贝拉米先生和艾夫斯太太险些自杀般地不检点了一场，所幸在最后一刻有所察觉。这也把我自己那桩罪无可赦的不检点，过于鲜活地带到了眼前，于是我随即护送邓恩小姐回了家，把她留在那里。”

“是——正是。那么，皮普斯先生，再问一两个问题。您第一次到小屋时，听见那女人的声音喊‘不敢碰我’，当时小屋的前后都在您注视之下，对吧？”

===Original===

“Would it have been possible for an automobile to be at any spot near the cottage while you were there without your attention being drawn to the fact?”

“It would have been absolutely impossible.”

“It could not have stood there without your seeing it?”

“Not possibly.”

“Nor have left without your hearing it?”

“Not possibly.”

“Did you hear or see such a car on that visit to the cottage, Mr. Phipps?”

“I saw no car and heard none.”

“Thank you, Mr. Phipps; that will be all.”

“Well, not quite all,” said Mr. Farr gently. Mr. Phipps shifted in his chair, his eyes under their dark brows luminous with apprehension. “Mr. Phipps, at what time did you reach your home on the night of the nineteenth of June?”

“I did not return to my home. It was closed, as my family﻿—my wife and my two little girls﻿—were staying at a little place on the Jersey coast called Blue Bay. I had taken a room at the Y.M.C.A.”

“At what time did you return to the Y.M.C.A.?”

“I did not return there,” said Mr. Phipps, in a voice so low that it was barely audible.

“You did not return to the Y.M.C.A.?”

“No. By the time that I had left Miss Dunne at her home I decided that it was too late to return to the Y.M.C.A. without rendering myself extremely conspicuous, and as I was not in the least sleepy, I decided that I would take a good walk, get a bite to eat at one of the handout places in the vicinity of the station, and catch the first train﻿—the four-forty-five﻿—to New York, where I could get a boat to Blue Bay and spend Sunday with my family.”

“You mean that you did not intend to go to bed at all?”

“I did not.”

“And you carried out this plan?”

“I did.”

“What time did you leave Miss Dunne at her home, Mr. Phipps?”

“At about quarter to one.”

“What time did you start from the Orchards for home?”

“We started from the lodge gates at a little before eleven.”

“How far is it from there to Miss Dunne’s home in Rosemont?”

“Just short of four miles.”

“It took you an hour and three-quarters to traverse four miles?”

“Yes. The last bus from Perrytown to Rosemont goes by Orchards at about quarter to eleven. We missed it by five or six minutes and were obliged to walk.”

“It took you over an hour and three quarters to walk less than four miles?”

“We walked slowly,” said Mr. Phipps.

“So it would seem. Now, did anyone see you leave Miss Dunne at her door, Mr. Phipps?”

“No one.”

===Chinese===

“您在那里的时候，一辆汽车若停在小屋附近任何一处，能不引起您的注意吗？”

“那是绝对不可能的。”

“它不可能停在那里而不被您看见？”

“不可能。”

“也不可能开走而不被您听见？”

“不可能。”

“那晚到小屋去的这一趲，您听见或看见过这样一辆车吗，皮普斯先生？”

“我没看见车，也没听见。”

“谢谢您，皮普斯先生；没有别的问题了。”

“嗯，还没完。”法尔先生温和地说。皮普斯先生在椅子里挪了挪身子，浓眉下的双眼因惶恐而发亮。“皮普斯先生，六月十九日夜里，您几点回到家的？”

“我没有回家。家里闭门无人，因为我的家人——我妻子和两个小女儿——住在泽西海岸一个叫蓝湾的小地方。我在基督教青年会开了个房间。”

“您几点回的基督教青年会？”

“我没有回去。”皮普斯先生说，声音低得几乎听不见。

“您没有回基督教青年会？”

“没有。把邓恩小姐送到家时，我想已经太晚，再回青年会会使自己格外惹眼；何况我一点也不困，便决意好好走一走，在车站附近哪家小饭摊吃点东西，赶头班车——四点四十五那班——去纽约，从那里搭船去蓝湾，跟家人过星期天。”

“您是说您压根没打算上床睡觉？”

“是的。”

“这个计划您实行了？”

“实行了。”

“您几点把邓恩小姐留在她家门口的，皮普斯先生？”

“一点差一刻左右。”

“你们几点从果园庄动身回家的？”
